[incumbent stderr] # Spectra Workflow Reference
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ## Use This Reference For
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] - Converting free-form reactive-system requirements into a first Spectra draft
[incumbent stderr] - Interpreting `spectra-cli.jar` results
[incumbent stderr] - Repairing parser errors without drifting away from the user's intent
[incumbent stderr] - Using counter-strategy and unrealizable-core diagnostics to repair unrealizable drafts
[incumbent stderr] - Checking well-separation after realizability and before synthesis
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ## Drafting Guidance
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] - Before writing a full draft, decompose the natural-language requirements into system boundary, environment inputs, system outputs, assumptions, guarantees, initial conditions, safety/update rules, liveness/justice requirements, and response/pattern requirements.
[incumbent stderr] - Add requirement groups incrementally in reasoning. After each group, check for ownership mistakes, hidden assumptions, contradictory initial conditions, over-strong guarantees, duplicate constraints, and unused variables.
[incumbent stderr] - Start with one named `spec`.
[incumbent stderr] - Declare environment variables with `env` and system variables with `sys`.
[incumbent stderr] - Encode assumptions with `asm` and guarantees with `gar`.
[incumbent stderr] - Add environment assumptions only when the description states or strongly implies an environment constraint.
[incumbent stderr] - Prefer a minimal complete model over speculative features.
[incumbent stderr] - Prefer the simplest Spectra construct that directly expresses the requirement.
[incumbent stderr] - Use advanced constructs such as `counter`, `monitor`, `pattern`, `regexp`, quantifiers, aggregates, and triggers only when the natural-language description clearly calls for them or they materially simplify the specification.
[incumbent stderr] - If the description leaves key semantics unstated, make the smallest explicit assumption and report it.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ## Validation And Synthesis Branches
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Use this check command:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ```bash
[incumbent stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file>
[incumbent stderr] ```
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Observed CLI result patterns in this repo:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] - Realizable: `Result: Specification is realizable`
[incumbent stderr] - Unrealizable: `Result: Specification is unrealizable`
[incumbent stderr] - Syntax failure: `Error: Could not prepare game input from Spectra file`
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Use this synthesis command only after a realizable result:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ```bash
[incumbent stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file> -s -o <output-dir>
[incumbent stderr] ```
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Observed synthesis success pattern:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] - `Result: Successfully synthesized a just-in-time controller in output folder`
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Run well-separation after a realizable result and before synthesis:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ```bash
[incumbent stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file> --well-separation
[incumbent stderr] ```
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Observed well-separation result patterns in this repo:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] - Well-separated: `Result: Specification is well-separated`
[incumbent stderr] - Non-well-separated: `Result: Specification is non-well-separated`
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] The CLI returns exit code 0 for both well-separated and non-well-separated results; branch on the result text or wrapper status.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ## Counter-Strategy Diagnostics
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] The current CLI exposes:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ```bash
[incumbent stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file> --counter-strategy
[incumbent stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file> --counter-strategy-jtlv-format
[incumbent stderr] ```
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Use these only after the normal check reports `Result: Specification is unrealizable`.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Read the counter-strategy as an environment-winning behavior that demonstrates why the system cannot force its guarantees. It is diagnostic evidence, not a target behavior to copy.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ## Unrealizable-Core Diagnostics
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] The wrapper exposes the unrealizable-core CLI stored under `assets/cli_with_unrealizable_core`:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ```bash
[incumbent stderr] python .agents/skills/respect-broker/scripts/run_spectra_cli.py --input <path-to-file> --unrealizable-core --timeout 120
[incumbent stderr] ```
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Use this only after the normal check reports `Result: Specification is unrealizable`.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Read the unrealizable core as one locally minimal set of guarantee source lines that is still unrealizable. It localizes a conflict but does not prove that the reported guarantees should be deleted or weakened.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ## Syntax-Repair Loop
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] 1. Save the draft as a `.spectra` file.
[incumbent stderr] 2. Run the CLI.
[incumbent stderr] 3. If parsing fails, extract the parser message, for example `missing ';' at 'gar'`.
[incumbent stderr] 4. Repair the file with the smallest syntax-preserving edit.
[incumbent stderr] 5. Repeat until the file is syntactically valid or the attempt budget is exhausted.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ## Unrealizability-Repair Loop
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] 1. Run the CLI and confirm the specification is syntactically valid but unrealizable.
[incumbent stderr] 2. Request a counter-strategy diagnostic.
[incumbent stderr] 3. Request an unrealizable-core diagnostic.
[incumbent stderr] 4. Identify a likely conflict between assumptions, guarantees, initial conditions, safety, liveness, or update rules. Use the core to focus attention on the implicated guarantee lines, and use the counter-strategy to understand a concrete environment-winning behavior.
[incumbent stderr] 5. Compare any candidate repair against the natural-language description.
[incumbent stderr] 6. Apply the candidate only when it is consistent with the description.
[incumbent stderr] 7. Re-run the normal CLI check.
[incumbent stderr] 8. Stop after 3 unrealizability-repair attempts or when all plausible repairs would contradict the description.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Common repair patterns:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] - Add a missing environment assumption only when the description says the environment is constrained.
[incumbent stderr] - Repair an initial condition that conflicts with an immediate response requirement.
[incumbent stderr] - Replace an accidental always-immediate response with a next-step response if the description allows delayed reaction.
[incumbent stderr] - Remove an over-specific liveness condition only when the description does not require that recurrence.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Common invalid repairs:
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] - Deleting a stated guarantee solely to make synthesis succeed.
[incumbent stderr] - Adding an assumption that forbids inputs the description explicitly permits.
[incumbent stderr] - Weakening "must always" into "may sometimes" without textual support.
[incumbent stderr] - Ignoring a named input/output or changing control ownership.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ## Well-Separation Loop
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] 1. Run the well-separation check only after the normal check reports `Result: Specification is realizable`.
[incumbent stderr] 2. If the result is non-well-separated, inspect environment assumptions first, especially assumptions that mention system variables or depend on system-controlled behavior.
[incumbent stderr] 3. Compare any candidate repair against the natural-language description.
[incumbent stderr] 4. Apply a repair only when it is consistent with the description.
[incumbent stderr] 5. Re-run the normal CLI check and then the well-separation check.
[incumbent stderr] 6. Stop after 3 well-separation-repair attempts or when all plausible repairs would contradict the description.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] ## Useful Local Sources
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] Use these files only as syntax and idiom references. Do not copy their behavior unless the natural-language requirements call for the same behavior, and do not use them as semantic or benchmark oracles.
[incumbent stderr] 
[incumbent stderr] - `assets/examples/A1_firstController/TrafficA1.spectra`
[incumbent stderr] - `assets/examples/A2_unrealizability/TrafficA2b.spectra`

[incumbent stderr] - `assets/examples/L2_defsArrays_solution/`

[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] # ReSpect Method Cross-Broker: Grammar-Guided CLI-Diagnosed Repair With Peer Disagreement Feedback
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Research Role
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] This skill implements a cross-agent ReSpect reconstruction condition:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Input: natural-language requirements for a reactive system.
[challenger stderr] - Output: a generated Spectra specification plus CLI validation, diagnosis, synthesis, broker feedback, and repair results.
[challenger stderr] - Initial syntax reference: `assets/grammar/Spectra.xtext`.
[challenger stderr] - Feedback sources before broker submission: `spectra-cli.jar` parser/realizability/synthesis/well-separation output and CLI counter-strategy and unrealizable-core diagnostics for unrealizable specifications.
[challenger stderr] - Feedback source after successful synthesis: disagreement feedback returned by `experiments/cross_broker.py`.
[challenger stderr] - Allowed repair signal: parser output, realizability status, well-separation status, counter-strategy output, unrealizable-core output, generated Spectra, the natural-language description, and broker disagreement feedback.
[challenger stderr] - Not allowed: `controller_tests`, generated controller tests, semantic equivalence checks against the benchmark, mutation checks, oracle-based tests, reading or comparing against the original/reference Spectra file, accepted dataset files, HOA distance results, or benchmark fixtures.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] The goal is to measure whether peer disagreement feedback can improve reconstruction after syntax and unrealizability handling, while avoiding the controller-test feedback source.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Best-Practice Drafting Order
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Before writing the first complete Spectra draft, perform a concise requirements decomposition:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] 1. Identify the system boundary and declare which named concepts are environment-controlled inputs and which are system-controlled outputs.
[challenger stderr] 2. Separate environment assumptions from system guarantees. Do not encode an assumption unless the natural-language description states or strongly implies the environment constraint.
[challenger stderr] 3. Classify each requirement as an initial condition, safety/update rule, liveness/justice requirement, or response/pattern requirement.
[challenger stderr] 4. Prefer the smallest kernel-level Spectra model that covers the stated requirements. Use advanced constructs such as patterns, monitors, counters, triggers, quantifiers, or arrays only when they express the natural-language requirement more directly or materially improve readability.
[challenger stderr] 5. Build the first draft incrementally in reasoning: add one coherent requirement group at a time, checking for ownership mistakes, hidden assumptions, contradictory initial conditions, over-strong guarantees, and duplicate or unused variables before saving `specs/00_initial.spectra`.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] This decomposition is a drafting discipline only. Do not read benchmark or source Spectra files, and do not run oracle-style checks.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Workflow
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] 1. Read `references/spectra-workflow.md` before drafting a new specification.
[challenger stderr] 2. Read `assets/grammar/Spectra.xtext` before drafting, and use it only as syntax guidance for valid Spectra constructs.
[challenger stderr] 3. Follow the best-practice drafting order above, then translate the user's natural-language description into a complete Spectra specification.
[challenger stderr] 4. Save the initial draft as `specs/00_initial.spectra` before validation.
[challenger stderr] 5. Initialize `repair_loops = 0`, `syntax_repair_loops = 0`, `unrealizable_repair_loops = 0`, `well_separation_repair_loops = 0`, `broker_repair_loops = 0`, `broker_witnesses_received = 0`, `accepted_by_self_rejected_by_peer_repaired = 0`, `accepted_by_self_rejected_by_peer_ignored = 0`, `rejected_by_self_accepted_by_peer_repaired = 0`, and `rejected_by_self_accepted_by_peer_ignored = 0`.
[challenger stderr] 6. Run `scripts/run_spectra_cli.py` on the saved file with an explicit timeout.
[challenger stderr] 7. If the result is `syntax_error`, inspect the parser message, increment both `repair_loops` and `syntax_repair_loops`, repair only the syntax, and rerun validation.
[challenger stderr] 8. After the syntax-repair phase ends and the run moves to the next phase, save the stable repaired specification as `specs/01_after_syntax_repair.spectra` and append one transition record to `repair_log.jsonl`.
[challenger stderr] 9. Limit syntax-repair loops to at most 3 attempts and report the last parser error if repair still fails.
[challenger stderr] 10. If the result is `timeout`, report the timeout and do not continue.
[challenger stderr] 11. If the result is `unrealizable`, request CLI diagnostics with `--counter-strategy` and save the diagnostic JSON, then request `--unrealizable-core` and save the core diagnostic JSON.
[challenger stderr] 12. Analyze the counter-strategy, the unrealizable core, and the natural-language description before changing the specification. Use the core only to localize guarantees participating in an unrealizability conflict; do not treat it as a deletion list.
[challenger stderr] 13. Repair unrealizability only if the proposed change is consistent with the natural-language description.
[challenger stderr] 14. Prefer minimal repairs that preserve stated requirements: fix overly strong initialization, add environment assumptions only when implied by the description, or relax/reshape a guarantee only when the description supports the weaker form.
[challenger stderr] 15. If every plausible repair would contradict the natural-language description, stop and report `blocked_by_nl_conflict = true`.
[challenger stderr] 16. After each unrealizability repair, increment both `repair_loops` and `unrealizable_repair_loops`, save the current working file, rerun validation, and continue from the appropriate branch.
[challenger stderr] 17. Limit unrealizability-repair loops to at most 3 attempts.
[challenger stderr] 18. After the unrealizability-repair phase ends and the run moves to well-separation or stops, save the stable repaired specification as `specs/02_after_unrealizable_repair.spectra` and append one transition record to `repair_log.jsonl`.
[challenger stderr] 19. If the specification is `realizable`, run the well-separation check before synthesis and save the wrapper JSON as `diagnostics/well-separation-<n>.json`.
[challenger stderr] 20. If the well-separation result is `non_well_separated`, inspect the generated Spectra and the natural-language description before changing the specification.
[challenger stderr] 21. Repair non-well-separation only by changing environment assumptions or related modeling choices that are inconsistent with, or not justified by, the natural-language description. Do not delete stated requirements, add artificial assumptions, or weaken guarantees unless the natural-language description supports that change.
[challenger stderr] 22. After each well-separation repair, increment both `repair_loops` and `well_separation_repair_loops`, save the current working file, rerun validation, rerun well-separation when realizable, and continue from the appropriate branch.
[challenger stderr] 23. Limit well-separation-repair loops to at most 3 attempts. If every plausible repair would contradict the natural-language description, stop and report `blocked_by_nl_conflict = true`.
[challenger stderr] 24. After the well-separation-repair phase ends and the run moves to synthesis or stops, save the stable repaired specification as `specs/03_after_well_separation_repair.spectra` and append one transition record to `repair_log.jsonl`.
[challenger stderr] 25. If the well-separation result is `well_separated`, run synthesis. If synthesis succeeds, set `cli_status = synthesized` and continue to broker submission.
[challenger stderr] 26. Submit the current synthesized Spectra file to the broker by running `experiments/cross_broker.py submit-and-wait` with the run id, current round id, agent id, spec path, expected agents, and timeout provided by the task prompt.
[challenger stderr] 27. Save the broker JSON response as `broker/feedback-<round>.json`. If the response contains a `feedback_file`, read that file and use it as the authoritative broker feedback for the round.
[challenger stderr] 28. If broker feedback is unavailable, has a non-ready status, or reports `semantic_relation = equivalent`, record the status and stop the broker loop.
[challenger stderr] 29. If broker feedback reports disagreement words, inspect both `accepted_by_you_rejected_by_peer` and `rejected_by_you_accepted_by_peer`. Treat them as peer disagreement evidence only, not as ground truth.
[challenger stderr] 30. Decide for each word whether the natural-language description supports a specification change. Count every word as either leading to repair or ignored, separately for each direction.
[challenger stderr] 31. If no word justifies a change, stop the broker loop and report that broker feedback was not applied.
[challenger stderr] 32. If at least one word justifies a change, make the minimal Spectra repair consistent with the natural-language description, increment both `repair_loops` and `broker_repair_loops`, save the file, rerun CLI validation, rerun well-separation when realizable, rerun synthesis, increment the broker round id, and submit the repaired Spectra to the broker again.
[challenger stderr] 33. After the broker-repair phase ends and the run moves to the next broker round or stops, save the stable repaired specification as `specs/04_after_broker_repair.spectra` and append one transition record to `repair_log.jsonl`.
[challenger stderr] 34. Limit broker-repair loops to at most 3 attempts.
[challenger stderr] 35. Save the final Spectra version as `final.spectra`. Always include the final method cross-broker result fields in the response.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Temporary File Handling
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Create a temporary working directory under the repository root, for example `tmp/spectra-runs/<timestamp>-<slug>/`.
[challenger stderr] - Use this stable artifact layout:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```text
[challenger stderr] tmp/spectra-runs/<timestamp>-<slug>/
[challenger stderr]   specs/
[challenger stderr]     00_initial.spectra
[challenger stderr]     01_after_syntax_repair.spectra
[challenger stderr]     02_after_unrealizable_repair.spectra
[challenger stderr]     03_after_well_separation_repair.spectra
[challenger stderr]     04_after_broker_repair.spectra
[challenger stderr]   diagnostics/
[challenger stderr]     unrealizable-<n>.json
[challenger stderr]     unrealizable-core-<n>.json
[challenger stderr]     well-separation-<n>.json
[challenger stderr]   broker/
[challenger stderr]     feedback-<round>.json
[challenger stderr]     feedback-detail-<round>.json
[challenger stderr]     repair-decisions-<round>.json
[challenger stderr]   final.spectra
[challenger stderr]   repair_log.jsonl
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Save only stable Spectra versions at phase boundaries: the initial draft, the specification that leaves each repair phase, and the final specification. Do not save every intermediate failed repair attempt inside a loop unless it is also the final state.
[challenger stderr] - Omit phase-specific spec files and repair-log entries for phases that did not run. For example, if no broker repair was attempted, do not create `specs/04_after_broker_repair.spectra`.
[challenger stderr] - Keep `final.spectra` as a copy of the last stable Spectra version and report `spectra_file` as that path.
[challenger stderr] - Append one JSON object per phase transition to `repair_log.jsonl`.
[challenger stderr] - Use this `repair_log.jsonl` schema consistently for cross-broker runs:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```json
[challenger stderr] {
[challenger stderr]   "version": "04_after_broker_repair",
[challenger stderr]   "phase": "broker_repair",
[challenger stderr]   "input_spec": "specs/03_after_well_separation_repair.spectra",
[challenger stderr]   "output_spec": "specs/04_after_broker_repair.spectra",
[challenger stderr]   "trigger": "broker_disagreement",
[challenger stderr]   "result_before": "synthesized",
[challenger stderr]   "result_after": "synthesized",
[challenger stderr]   "repair_loops_total": 2,
[challenger stderr]   "syntax_repair_loops": 0,
[challenger stderr]   "unrealizable_repair_loops": 1,
[challenger stderr]   "well_separation_repair_loops": 0,
[challenger stderr]   "test_repair_loops": 0,
[challenger stderr]   "broker_repair_loops": 1,
[challenger stderr]   "diagnostic_files": ["broker/feedback-1.json", "broker/repair-decisions-1.json"],
[challenger stderr]   "notes": "Minimal NL-consistent broker-feedback repair summary."
[challenger stderr] }
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Save the JSON output of each counter-strategy wrapper call as `diagnostics/unrealizable-<n>.json`. The actual counter-strategy text is preserved inside that JSON object's `raw_output` field.
[challenger stderr] - Save the JSON output of each unrealizable-core wrapper call as `diagnostics/unrealizable-core-<n>.json`. The reported `unrealizable_core_lines` identify source lines for guarantees in one locally minimal unrealizable core. Treat these lines as localization evidence, not as permission to remove or weaken the guarantees.
[challenger stderr] - Save the JSON output of each well-separation wrapper call as `diagnostics/well-separation-<n>.json`. The actual CLI text is preserved inside that JSON object's `raw_output` field.
[challenger stderr] - Save broker responses as `broker/feedback-<round>.json`.
[challenger stderr] - If the broker response references `feedback_file`, read that file and save a copy as `broker/feedback-detail-<round>.json` before making any repair decision.
[challenger stderr] - Save a concise repair-decision log as `broker/repair-decisions-<round>.json`, including per-word decisions and counters.
[challenger stderr] - Keep the final `.spectra` file and synthesis output long enough for inspection.
[challenger stderr] - Do not overwrite unrelated files.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Validation Commands
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Use the bundled wrapper instead of calling the jar directly:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] python .agents/skills/respect-broker/scripts/run_spectra_cli.py --input <path-to-file> --timeout 120
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] For counter-strategy diagnostics after an `unrealizable` result:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] python .agents/skills/respect-broker/scripts/run_spectra_cli.py --input <path-to-file> --counter-strategy --timeout 120
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] For unrealizable-core diagnostics after an `unrealizable` result:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] python .agents/skills/respect-broker/scripts/run_spectra_cli.py --input <path-to-file> --unrealizable-core --timeout 120
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] For well-separation after a `realizable` result and before synthesis:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] python .agents/skills/respect-broker/scripts/run_spectra_cli.py --input <path-to-file> --well-separation --timeout 120
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] The wrapper returns `status = well_separated` or `status = non_well_separated`. The underlying CLI returns exit code 0 for both outcomes, so use the normalized `status`, not the process exit code, to branch.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Use JTLV text format only if the default counter-strategy output is not useful:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] python .agents/skills/respect-broker/scripts/run_spectra_cli.py --input <path-to-file> --counter-strategy-jtlv-format --timeout 120
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] For synthesis after a `realizable` result:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] python .agents/skills/respect-broker/scripts/run_spectra_cli.py --input <path-to-file> --synthesize --output-dir <path-to-output-dir> --timeout 120
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Broker Command
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Use the repository broker after successful synthesis. The task prompt must provide `run_id`, `agent_id`, and `round_id`. Use `agent_a` or `agent_b` unless the orchestrator provides a different expected-agent set.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] python experiments/cross_broker.py submit-and-wait --run-id <run-id> --round <round-id> --agent <agent-id> --spec <path-to-file> --timeout 600
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] If the orchestrator provides `expected_agents`, pass them through:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] python experiments/cross_broker.py submit-and-wait --run-id <run-id> --round <round-id> --agent <agent-id> --spec <path-to-file> --expected-agents agent_a agent_b --timeout 600
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Treat broker responses as structured experiment feedback:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - `ready`: broker feedback is available. Read the referenced `feedback_file` when present.
[challenger stderr] - `timeout`: peer feedback was unavailable; do not invent broker feedback.
[challenger stderr] - `comparison_failed`, `alphabet_mismatch`, or other non-ready statuses: report the broker status and do not continue to broker-based repair.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] The skill-facing feedback file has this shape:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```json
[challenger stderr] {
[challenger stderr]   "status": "ready",
[challenger stderr]   "semantic_relation": "equivalent|different|unknown",
[challenger stderr]   "accepted_by_you_rejected_by_peer": [],
[challenger stderr]   "rejected_by_you_accepted_by_peer": [],
[challenger stderr]   "witness_count": 0,
[challenger stderr]   "comparison_file": "..."
[challenger stderr] }
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] `accepted_by_you_rejected_by_peer` contains ultimately-periodic words accepted by the current generated Spectra specification but rejected by the peer specification. `rejected_by_you_accepted_by_peer` contains ultimately-periodic words rejected by the current generated Spectra specification but accepted by the peer specification. Each word is represented as `prefix` and `loop`, where the loop repeats forever.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Broker words are disagreement evidence, not oracle counterexamples. The peer specification was produced by another agent and may be wrong. Do not treat peer-accepted behavior as required or peer-rejected behavior as forbidden unless the natural-language requirements support that conclusion.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Method Cross-Broker Boundaries
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Use the syntax-repair and unrealizability-repair discipline defined in this skill.
[challenger stderr] - Use `assets/grammar/Spectra.xtext` as a syntax reference, not as semantic feedback or a repair oracle.
[challenger stderr] - Do not run `controller_tests`.
[challenger stderr] - Do not create or run NL-guided controller tests.
[challenger stderr] - Do not run generated semantic tests, trace tests, benchmark-specific oracle checks, mutation checks, or equivalence checks.
[challenger stderr] - Do not open `source_spectra_file`, accepted dataset files, HOA exports, distance results, or benchmark fixtures for the current instance.
[challenger stderr] - Do not make a specification realizable by deleting stated requirements without a natural-language justification.
[challenger stderr] - Do not weaken a guarantee merely because it appears in the counter-strategy; first decide whether the weaker behavior is allowed by the description.
[challenger stderr] - Do not add environment assumptions that shift responsibility to the environment unless the description states or strongly implies that assumption.
[challenger stderr] - Do not compare the generated Spectra file against an original/reference Spectra file.
[challenger stderr] - Do not treat broker feedback as proof that the generated Spectra is wrong.
[challenger stderr] - Do not repair solely to match the peer specification. Repair only to better satisfy the natural-language description.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Unrealizability Repair Rules
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Treat the counter-strategy as diagnostic evidence, not as permission to rewrite the task.
[challenger stderr] - Treat the unrealizable core as diagnostic localization evidence, not as permission to delete, weaken, or ignore the reported guarantees.
[challenger stderr] - State the suspected conflict before editing.
[challenger stderr] - Check each proposed repair against the natural-language description:
[challenger stderr]   - `consistent`: apply the minimal edit and retry.
[challenger stderr]   - `unclear`: prefer a smaller edit or stop if the assumption would be material.
[challenger stderr]   - `contradicts`: do not apply the edit.
[challenger stderr] - Preserve all explicitly described inputs, outputs, initial conditions, safety requirements, liveness requirements, and update rules unless the description itself leaves room for the change.
[challenger stderr] - Keep a concise repair log for each unrealizability loop.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Well-Separation Repair Rules
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Treat `non_well_separated` as diagnostic evidence that the system may be able to force the environment to violate its own assumptions.
[challenger stderr] - Inspect environment assumptions first, especially assumptions that mention system variables or constrain the environment based on system-controlled behavior.
[challenger stderr] - Repair only when the natural-language description supports the change.
[challenger stderr] - Prefer removing or reshaping unjustified environment assumptions over adding new assumptions.
[challenger stderr] - Do not delete stated guarantees or weaken required behavior solely to make the well-separation check pass.
[challenger stderr] - If the natural-language description genuinely requires a non-well-separated environment contract, stop and report `blocked_by_nl_conflict = true`.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Broker Feedback Handling
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] When broker feedback is ready:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] 1. Read the broker JSON response from stdout and save it as `broker/feedback-<round>.json`.
[challenger stderr] 2. If the JSON contains `feedback_file`, read that file and save a copy as `broker/feedback-detail-<round>.json`.
[challenger stderr] 3. Set `broker_feedback_status` to the feedback `status`.
[challenger stderr] 4. Set `broker_witnesses_received` to the total `witness_count`.
[challenger stderr] 5. If `semantic_relation = equivalent`, set `broker_repair_decision = equivalent` and stop.
[challenger stderr] 6. If no feedback file exists, the status is not `ready`, or the semantic relation is `unknown`, set `broker_repair_decision = no_repair` and stop.
[challenger stderr] 7. For each word in `accepted_by_you_rejected_by_peer`, decide whether the natural-language description says the behavior should be rejected. If yes, count it in `accepted_by_self_rejected_by_peer_repaired`; otherwise count it in `accepted_by_self_rejected_by_peer_ignored`.
[challenger stderr] 8. For each word in `rejected_by_you_accepted_by_peer`, decide whether the natural-language description says the behavior should be accepted. If yes, count it in `rejected_by_self_accepted_by_peer_repaired`; otherwise count it in `rejected_by_self_accepted_by_peer_ignored`.
[challenger stderr] 9. Save the per-word decisions and the counters as `broker/repair-decisions-<round>.json`.
[challenger stderr] 10. If no word supports a change, set `broker_repair_decision = ignored_feedback` and stop.
[challenger stderr] 11. If one or more words support a change, apply one minimal coherent repair that addresses the justified feedback without contradicting the natural-language description. Set `broker_repair_decision = repaired`, increment `repair_loops` and `broker_repair_loops`, rerun validation and synthesis, then submit the repaired Spectra in the next broker round.
[challenger stderr] 12. Stop after 3 broker-repair loops even if further disagreement remains.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] For `accepted_by_you_rejected_by_peer`, a justified repair usually means strengthening or correcting the generated Spectra so that the disputed behavior is no longer accepted. For `rejected_by_you_accepted_by_peer`, a justified repair usually means relaxing or correcting the generated Spectra so that the disputed behavior becomes accepted. In both directions, the natural-language description is the deciding authority, not the peer.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Final Response Format
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] For study runs, keep the final response compact and include:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```text
[challenger stderr] cli_status: <syntax_error|unrealizable|realizable|synthesized|timeout|unknown>
[challenger stderr] repair_loops: <number>
[challenger stderr] syntax_repair_loops: <number>
[challenger stderr] unrealizable_repair_loops: <number>
[challenger stderr] well_separation_repair_loops: <number>
[challenger stderr] broker_repair_loops: <number>
[challenger stderr] timeout_seconds: <number>
[challenger stderr] used_counter_strategy: <true|false>
[challenger stderr] used_unrealizable_core: <true|false>
[challenger stderr] unrealizable_core_file: <path or none>
[challenger stderr] unrealizable_core_size: <number or none>
[challenger stderr] unrealizable_core_lines: <JSON array of line numbers, or []>
[challenger stderr] well_separation_status: <well_separated|non_well_separated|not_checked|unknown>
[challenger stderr] blocked_by_nl_conflict: <true|false>
[challenger stderr] diagnostic_file: <path or none>
[challenger stderr] well_separation_file: <path or none>
[challenger stderr] broker_feedback_status: <ready|timeout|comparison_failed|alphabet_mismatch|none|other>
[challenger stderr] broker_feedback_file: <path or none>
[challenger stderr] broker_witnesses_received: <number>
[challenger stderr] broker_repair_decision: <equivalent|no_repair|ignored_feedback|repaired|none>
[challenger stderr] accepted_by_self_rejected_by_peer_repaired: <number>
[challenger stderr] accepted_by_self_rejected_by_peer_ignored: <number>
[challenger stderr] rejected_by_self_accepted_by_peer_repaired: <number>
[challenger stderr] rejected_by_self_accepted_by_peer_ignored: <number>
[challenger stderr] spectra_file: <path>
[challenger stderr] controller_output_dir: <path or none>
[challenger stderr] artifact_dir: <path>
[challenger stderr] repair_log_file: <path>
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Bundled Resources

[challenger stderr] - `references/spectra-workflow.md`: Spectra examples, CLI result patterns, and drafting/repair guidance.
[challenger stderr] - `assets/examples/LanguageFeatures/`: Compact `.spectra` syntax examples for less common Spectra language constructs.

[challenger stderr] - `experiments/cross_broker.py`: Repository-level broker used after successful synthesis to synchronize peer disagreement feedback.

[challenger stderr]  succeeded in 2716ms:
[challenger stderr] # Spectra Workflow Reference
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Use This Reference For
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Converting free-form reactive-system requirements into a first Spectra draft
[challenger stderr] - Interpreting `spectra-cli.jar` results
[challenger stderr] - Repairing parser errors without drifting away from the user's intent
[challenger stderr] - Using counter-strategy and unrealizable-core diagnostics to repair unrealizable drafts
[challenger stderr] - Checking well-separation after realizability and before synthesis
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Drafting Guidance
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Before writing a full draft, decompose the natural-language requirements into system boundary, environment inputs, system outputs, assumptions, guarantees, initial conditions, safety/update rules, liveness/justice requirements, and response/pattern requirements.
[challenger stderr] - Add requirement groups incrementally in reasoning. After each group, check for ownership mistakes, hidden assumptions, contradictory initial conditions, over-strong guarantees, duplicate constraints, and unused variables.
[challenger stderr] - Start with one named `spec`.
[challenger stderr] - Declare environment variables with `env` and system variables with `sys`.
[challenger stderr] - Encode assumptions with `asm` and guarantees with `gar`.
[challenger stderr] - Add environment assumptions only when the description states or strongly implies an environment constraint.
[challenger stderr] - Prefer a minimal complete model over speculative features.
[challenger stderr] - Prefer the simplest Spectra construct that directly expresses the requirement.
[challenger stderr] - Use advanced constructs such as `counter`, `monitor`, `pattern`, `regexp`, quantifiers, aggregates, and triggers only when the natural-language description clearly calls for them or they materially simplify the specification.
[challenger stderr] - If the description leaves key semantics unstated, make the smallest explicit assumption and report it.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Validation And Synthesis Branches
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Use this check command:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file>
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Observed CLI result patterns in this repo:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Realizable: `Result: Specification is realizable`
[challenger stderr] - Unrealizable: `Result: Specification is unrealizable`
[challenger stderr] - Syntax failure: `Error: Could not prepare game input from Spectra file`
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Use this synthesis command only after a realizable result:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file> -s -o <output-dir>
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Observed synthesis success pattern:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - `Result: Successfully synthesized a just-in-time controller in output folder`
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Run well-separation after a realizable result and before synthesis:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file> --well-separation
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Observed well-separation result patterns in this repo:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Well-separated: `Result: Specification is well-separated`
[challenger stderr] - Non-well-separated: `Result: Specification is non-well-separated`
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] The CLI returns exit code 0 for both well-separated and non-well-separated results; branch on the result text or wrapper status.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Counter-Strategy Diagnostics
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] The current CLI exposes:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file> --counter-strategy
[challenger stderr] java -jar spectra-cli.jar -i <path-to-file> --counter-strategy-jtlv-format
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Use these only after the normal check reports `Result: Specification is unrealizable`.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Read the counter-strategy as an environment-winning behavior that demonstrates why the system cannot force its guarantees. It is diagnostic evidence, not a target behavior to copy.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Unrealizable-Core Diagnostics
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] The wrapper exposes the unrealizable-core CLI stored under `assets/cli_with_unrealizable_core`:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ```bash
[challenger stderr] python .agents/skills/respect-broker/scripts/run_spectra_cli.py --input <path-to-file> --unrealizable-core --timeout 120
[challenger stderr] ```
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Use this only after the normal check reports `Result: Specification is unrealizable`.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Read the unrealizable core as one locally minimal set of guarantee source lines that is still unrealizable. It localizes a conflict but does not prove that the reported guarantees should be deleted or weakened.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Syntax-Repair Loop
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] 1. Save the draft as a `.spectra` file.
[challenger stderr] 2. Run the CLI.
[challenger stderr] 3. If parsing fails, extract the parser message, for example `missing ';' at 'gar'`.
[challenger stderr] 4. Repair the file with the smallest syntax-preserving edit.
[challenger stderr] 5. Repeat until the file is syntactically valid or the attempt budget is exhausted.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Unrealizability-Repair Loop
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] 1. Run the CLI and confirm the specification is syntactically valid but unrealizable.
[challenger stderr] 2. Request a counter-strategy diagnostic.
[challenger stderr] 3. Request an unrealizable-core diagnostic.
[challenger stderr] 4. Identify a likely conflict between assumptions, guarantees, initial conditions, safety, liveness, or update rules. Use the core to focus attention on the implicated guarantee lines, and use the counter-strategy to understand a concrete environment-winning behavior.
[challenger stderr] 5. Compare any candidate repair against the natural-language description.
[challenger stderr] 6. Apply the candidate only when it is consistent with the description.
[challenger stderr] 7. Re-run the normal CLI check.
[challenger stderr] 8. Stop after 3 unrealizability-repair attempts or when all plausible repairs would contradict the description.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Common repair patterns:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Add a missing environment assumption only when the description says the environment is constrained.
[challenger stderr] - Repair an initial condition that conflicts with an immediate response requirement.
[challenger stderr] - Replace an accidental always-immediate response with a next-step response if the description allows delayed reaction.
[challenger stderr] - Remove an over-specific liveness condition only when the description does not require that recurrence.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Common invalid repairs:
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] - Deleting a stated guarantee solely to make synthesis succeed.
[challenger stderr] - Adding an assumption that forbids inputs the description explicitly permits.
[challenger stderr] - Weakening "must always" into "may sometimes" without textual support.
[challenger stderr] - Ignoring a named input/output or changing control ownership.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Well-Separation Loop
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] 1. Run the well-separation check only after the normal check reports `Result: Specification is realizable`.
[challenger stderr] 2. If the result is non-well-separated, inspect environment assumptions first, especially assumptions that mention system variables or depend on system-controlled behavior.
[challenger stderr] 3. Compare any candidate repair against the natural-language description.
[challenger stderr] 4. Apply a repair only when it is consistent with the description.
[challenger stderr] 5. Re-run the normal CLI check and then the well-separation check.
[challenger stderr] 6. Stop after 3 well-separation-repair attempts or when all plausible repairs would contradict the description.
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] ## Useful Local Sources
[challenger stderr] 
[challenger stderr] Use these files only as syntax and idiom references. Do not copy their behavior unless the natural-language requirements call for the same behavior, and do not use them as semantic or benchmark oracles.
[challenger stderr]
